{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-6","id":"msg_01MocRw5RhZsyGbhKBVvtAfe","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_014x3mPWUZSwsNcCrZFW2J9r","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/documentation.md","content":"# Migration Guide: Envoy External Processing Filter (ext_proc) v1.30 → v1.31\n\n## Overview\n\nEnvoy v1.31.0 (released July 19, 2024) introduces several significant changes to the External Processing (ext_proc) HTTP filter. The release adds two major new capabilities — **Observability Mode** and the **`route_cache_action` enum** — while also changing the HTTP timeout error response code, introducing gRPC sidestream flow control, and making a wire-level type change in the service API for `ImmediateResponse.body`.\n\nMost operators will need to update their configurations to use `route_cache_action` in place of `disable_clear_route_cache`, and should be aware that ext_proc timeout errors now return HTTP 504 instead of 500.\n\n### Summary of Major Changes\n\n| Area | Change Type | Impact |\n|------|-------------|--------|\n| `ImmediateResponse.body` type changed from `string` to `bytes` | Breaking (proto wire) | Ext_proc server implementations must update generated code |\n| `disable_clear_route_cache` deprecated in favor of `route_cache_action` enum | Deprecation + behavior change | Config update recommended; cannot use both simultaneously |\n| Timeout errors now return HTTP 504 Gateway Timeout instead of 500 | Behavior change | Update client error-handling and alerting |\n| New `observability_mode` field (supersedes `async_mode`) | New feature | Optional adoption |\n| New `deferred_close_timeout` field for observability mode | New feature | Optional adoption |\n| New `route_cache_action` enum with `DEFAULT`, `CLEAR`, `RETAIN` values | New feature + deprecation | Replaces `disable_clear_route_cache` |\n| gRPC sidestream flow control via runtime flag | New feature | Enabled by default; can be disabled |\n| CEL attribute type handling changed (`kInt64`/`kUint64` replace `kNullType`/`kDouble`) | Behavior change | Affects `request_attributes`/`response_attributes` users |\n| Tracing context propagation to ext_proc gRPC streams | Bug fix / new behavior | No config change required |\n\n---\n\n## Breaking Changes\n\n### 1. `ImmediateResponse.body` Type Changed: `string` → `bytes`\n\n**File:** `api/envoy/service/ext_proc/v3/external_processor.proto`, line 342\n\nThe `body` field in the `ImmediateResponse` message changed its protobuf type from `string` to `bytes`.\n\n**v1.30 proto definition:**\n```protobuf\nmessage ImmediateResponse {\n  type.v3.HttpStatus status = 1;\n  HeaderMutation headers = 2;\n  // The message body to return with the response.\n  string body = 3;       // <-- was string\n  GrpcStatus grpc_status = 4;\n  string details = 5;\n}\n```\n\n**v1.31 proto definition:**\n```protobuf\nmessage ImmediateResponse {\n  type.v3.HttpStatus status = 1;\n  HeaderMutation headers = 2;\n  // The message body to return with the response.\n  bytes body = 3;        // <-- now bytes\n  GrpcStatus grpc_status = 4;\n  string details = 5;\n}\n```\n\n**Impact:** Although protobuf field number 3 remains the same (wire-compatible at the encoding level), the generated code in your ext_proc server will change type. In most languages:\n\n- **Go:** The field type changes from `string` to `[]byte`. Assignment code like `resp.Body = \"error message\"` must become `resp.Body = []byte(\"error message\")`.\n- **Java:** Changes from `String` to `ByteString`. Use `ByteString.copyFromUtf8(\"error message\")`.\n- **Python:** Changes from `str` to `bytes`. Use `b\"error message\"`.\n- **C++:** Changes from `std::string` to a bytes-typed field (still `std::string` in C++ protobuf, but semantically different).\n\n**Motivation:** HTTP response bodies are arbitrary binary content, not necessarily valid UTF-8. Using `bytes` is more correct and removes ambiguity.\n\n**Action Required:** Regenerate your gRPC/protobuf client stubs from the updated `.proto` files and update any ext_proc server code that sets `ImmediateResponse.body`.\n\n---\n\n### 2. `disable_clear_route_cache` and `route_cache_action` Cannot Be Set Simultaneously\n\n**File:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.cc`\n\nIn v1.31, if both `disable_clear_route_cache` (set to `true`) and `route_cache_action` (set to any non-`DEFAULT` value) are present in the same `ExternalProcessor` configuration, Envoy will **throw a configuration error at startup**:\n\n```\nEnvoyException: disable_clear_route_cache and route_cache_action can not be set to none-default at the same time.\n```\n\n**v1.30 config (valid in v1.30, throws in v1.31 if both non-default):**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: ext_proc_cluster\n    disable_clear_route_cache: true\n    route_cache_action: RETAIN   # CONFLICT: both fields now active\n```\n\n**Action Required:** Use only one of the two fields. See the [Deprecated Features](#deprecated-features) section for the recommended migration path.\n\n---\n\n## Deprecated Features\n\n### 1. `disable_clear_route_cache` Deprecated in Favor of `route_cache_action`\n\n**Proto field:** `ExternalProcessor.disable_clear_route_cache` (field 11)\n**Replacement:** `ExternalProcessor.route_cache_action` (field 18, new in v1.31)\n**Files:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`, `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/processor_state.cc`\n\nThe boolean `disable_clear_route_cache` field is now annotated for future promotion to a `oneof` and is superseded by the new `route_cache_action` enum. The field still works in v1.31 but Envoy internally converts it: if `disable_clear_route_cache: true` is set, it is treated as `route_cache_action: RETAIN`.\n\nThe new `RouteCacheAction` enum provides three options:\n\n| Value | Behavior |\n|-------|----------|\n| `DEFAULT` (0) | Clear the route cache only when the ext_proc response sets `clear_route_cache: true` AND there is a header mutation in the response. This is the default. |\n| `CLEAR` (1) | Always clear the route cache whenever there is a header mutation, regardless of the `clear_route_cache` flag in the ext_proc response. |\n| `RETAIN` (2) | Never clear the route cache. Equivalent to the old `disable_clear_route_cache: true`. Increments the `clear_route_cache_disabled` stat if the response tried to clear it. |\n\nAdditionally, in v1.31 the `DEFAULT` behavior itself changed slightly: the route cache is now only cleared when **both** the ext_proc response's `clear_route_cache` field is `true` **and** there is at least one header mutation. In v1.30, any response with `clear_route_cache: true` (even without header mutations) would trigger a cache clear.\n\n**v1.30 config:**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: ext_proc_cluster\n    disable_clear_route_cache: true\n```\n\n**v1.31 recommended config (equivalent behavior):**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: ext_proc_cluster\n    route_cache_action: RETAIN\n```\n\n**Migration advice:**\n- Replace `disable_clear_route_cache: true` → `route_cache_action: RETAIN`\n- Replace `disable_clear_route_cache: false` (or omitting the field) → `route_cache_action: DEFAULT` (or omit, same result)\n- To always force a route re-computation on header mutations: use `route_cache_action: CLEAR`\n\n---\n\n## New Features\n\n### 1. Observability Mode (`observability_mode`)\n\n**Proto field:** `ExternalProcessor.observability_mode` (field 17, existed in v1.30 but formalized in v1.31)\n**New field:** `ExternalProcessor.deferred_close_timeout` (field 19, new in v1.31)\n**Files:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`, `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.h`, `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.cc`\n\nObservability mode (also called \"Send and Go\" mode) allows the ext_proc filter to forward all configured processing steps to the external processor **without pausing the filter chain**. Envoy sends each message and immediately continues processing — it does not wait for a response. This is the recommended replacement for the unimplemented `async_mode` concept.\n\n**Characteristics of observability mode:**\n1. Only `STREAMED` body processing mode is supported. Other body modes (`BUFFERED`, `BUFFERED_PARTIAL`) are silently ignored. `NONE` (skip body) still works.\n2. The external processor should not send responses; any responses sent are ignored.\n3. `message_timeout` does not apply in this mode.\n4. The external processor may close the stream to signal it no longer needs data.\n5. The gRPC stream is closed **after a deferred timeout** (default: 5 seconds) rather than immediately on filter destruction, to allow in-flight messages to drain.\n\n**New `deferred_close_timeout` field:**\n\nIn v1.31, when `observability_mode` is `true`, the gRPC stream to the ext_proc server is not closed immediately when the HTTP request/response completes. Instead, it is closed after a configurable delay, giving the ext_proc server time to receive and process all buffered messages.\n\nDefault value: **5000 milliseconds (5 seconds)**\n\n**Example configuration:**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: ext_proc_observer_cluster\n    observability_mode: true\n    deferred_close_timeout: 10s    # Allow 10s for stream drain (default: 5s)\n    processing_mode:\n      request_header_mode: SEND\n      response_header_mode: SEND\n      request_body_mode: STREAMED  # Only STREAMED is supported in observability mode\n      response_body_mode: STREAMED\n```\n\n**Flow control warning:** Flow control to prevent fast senders from overwhelming a slower ext_proc server in observability mode is not yet fully implemented. Use this feature at your own discretion, especially under high traffic. (Tracked at [envoy issue #33319](https://github.com/envoyproxy/envoy/issues/33319).)\n\n---\n\n### 2. `route_cache_action` Enum\n\n**Proto field:** `ExternalProcessor.route_cache_action` (field 18)\n**File:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`\n\nSee the [Deprecated Features](#deprecated-features) section for full details. The key new capability is `CLEAR` mode, which lets you configure ext_proc to always re-evaluate routes after header modifications, without requiring the ext_proc server to set the `clear_route_cache` flag in each response.\n\n**Example — always clear route cache:**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: ext_proc_cluster\n    route_cache_action: CLEAR\n```\n\n---\n\n### 3. gRPC Sidestream Flow Control\n\n**Runtime flag:** `envoy.reloadable_features.grpc_side_stream_flow_control`\n**Files:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/client_impl.h`, `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/client_impl.cc`, `source/common/runtime/runtime_features.cc`\n\nv1.31 adds flow control support to the bidirectional gRPC stream between Envoy and the ext_proc server. When enabled (default: `true`), Envoy sets watermark callbacks on the gRPC stream and removes them when the stream closes, preventing the ext_proc stream from being flooded with data faster than the external server can process it.\n\nThis feature is enabled by default and requires no configuration. It can be disabled via the runtime flag if it causes issues:\n\n```yaml\n# Bootstrap config to disable (if needed)\nlayered_runtime:\n  layers:\n  - name: static_layer\n    static_layer:\n      envoy.reloadable_features.grpc_side_stream_flow_control: false\n```\n\n---\n\n### 4. Tracing Context Propagation to ext_proc gRPC Streams\n\n**File:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.cc`\n**Changelog:** `changelogs/current.yaml` (minor behavior change)\n\nIn v1.31, tracing context headers (e.g., `traceparent`, `x-b3-traceid`) are now correctly propagated when Envoy opens the gRPC stream to the ext_proc server. In v1.30 and earlier, these headers were not set on the gRPC sidestream connection.\n\nThis change affects both the Envoy gRPC client and the Google C++ gRPC client. No configuration changes are required, but ext_proc server operators may now observe tracing headers that were previously absent. This enables distributed tracing to span the ext_proc side-channel.\n\n---\n\n### 5. New Test Infrastructure: Tracing Test Filter\n\n**Files:** `test/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/tracer_test_filter.cc`, `test/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/tracer_test_filter.proto` (new in v1.31)\n\nA new tracing test filter and matching utilities test (`matching_utils_test.cc`) have been added. These are test-only components and do not affect production behavior, but signal that tracing integration is now formally tested for ext_proc.\n\n---\n\n## Behavioral Changes\n\n### 1. Timeout Errors Now Return HTTP 504 Instead of HTTP 500\n\n**File:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.cc`\n**Changelog:** `changelogs/current.yaml`, minor behavior change section\n\nWhen the ext_proc filter's `message_timeout` expires before the external processor responds, Envoy now sends an HTTP **504 Gateway Timeout** response to the downstream client. Previously, this error returned HTTP **500 Internal Server Error**.\n\n**Impact:**\n- Downstream clients that check for `500` errors to detect ext_proc timeout failures must now also check (or switch to checking) for `504`.\n- Alert rules and dashboards monitoring for `5xx` errors are not affected — both codes are counted as server errors.\n- The `RESPONSE_CODE_DETAILS` access log field for these errors will reflect the gateway timeout.\n\n**No configuration change is required**, but you should:\n- Update any client-side retry logic that specifically matches on HTTP 500 for ext_proc timeouts.\n- Update monitoring dashboards and SLO definitions that specifically count ext_proc-induced HTTP 500s.\n\n---\n\n### 2. Route Cache Clear Now Requires Header Mutation (DEFAULT mode)\n\n**File:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/processor_state.cc`\n\nIn v1.30, if an ext_proc response set `clear_route_cache: true`, the route cache was cleared regardless of whether the response also contained a header mutation.\n\nIn v1.31 with `route_cache_action: DEFAULT` (the default), the route cache is **only cleared when both conditions are true:**\n1. The ext_proc response sets `clear_route_cache: true`, **and**\n2. The response includes at least one header mutation (`has_header_mutation()` returns true).\n\nIf an ext_proc response sets `clear_route_cache: true` but has no header mutations, the cache clear is skipped and the `clear_route_cache_ignored` counter is incremented.\n\n**Motivation:** Clearing the route cache is only meaningful when headers have changed. A response requesting cache clear with no mutations is likely a bug in the ext_proc server.\n\n**Action Required:** If your ext_proc server sets `clear_route_cache: true` on responses without header mutations, you need to either:\n- Add a header mutation to the response (even a no-op one), or\n- Switch to `route_cache_action: CLEAR` to always clear on header mutations regardless of the flag, or\n- Fix the ext_proc server to not set `clear_route_cache` when there are no header mutations.\n\n---\n\n### 3. CEL Attribute Value Type Handling Changed\n\n**File:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/matching_utils.cc`\n\nThe CEL expression evaluation for `request_attributes` and `response_attributes` changed how it maps CEL value types to protobuf `Value` types:\n\n| CEL Type | v1.30 behavior | v1.31 behavior |\n|----------|---------------|---------------|\n| `kNullType` | Sets `null_value` in proto Value | **Removed** — no longer handled |\n| `kDouble` | Sets `number_value` via `DoubleOrDie()` | **Removed** — replaced by integer handling |\n| `kInt64` | Not explicitly handled (fell through) | Sets `number_value` via `Int64OrDie()` |\n| `kUint64` | Not explicitly handled (fell through) | Sets `number_value` via `Uint64OrDie()` |\n\n**Impact:**\n- Attributes with CEL expressions that evaluate to `null` or floating-point `double` values will no longer produce `number_value` or `null_value` fields in the `attributes` map. They will fall through to string conversion instead.\n- Attributes that evaluate to `int64` or `uint64` values now correctly appear as `number_value` in the attributes map sent to the ext_proc server.\n\nThis is primarily relevant for operators using numeric or null-returning CEL expressions in `request_attributes` or `response_attributes`.\n\n---\n\n### 4. Stream Lifecycle and `notifyFilterDestroy()`\n\n**Files:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/client.h`, `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/client_impl.h`, `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/client_impl.cc`\n\nInternally, the gRPC stream between Envoy and the ext_proc server is now managed by a thread-local `ThreadLocalStreamManager` rather than being owned directly by the filter instance as a `unique_ptr`. The `ExternalProcessorStream` interface gained a new `notifyFilterDestroy()` method that must be called when the filter is destroyed, to prevent the stream from making callbacks into a destroyed filter object.\n\nThe `callbacks_` member in `ExternalProcessorStreamImpl` changed from a raw reference (`ExternalProcessorCallbacks&`) to an optional reference (`OptRef<ExternalProcessorCallbacks>`), which is reset to empty when `notifyFilterDestroy()` is called.\n\n**Impact on custom ext_proc client implementations:** If you have implemented a custom `ExternalProcessorClient` (unlikely for most operators, but possible for testing), you must implement the new `notifyFilterDestroy()` pure virtual method.\n\n---\n\n## Migration Steps\n\n### Step 1: Update Protobuf Generated Code\n\nRe-generate your ext_proc server's gRPC stubs from the updated `.proto` files:\n\n```bash\n# Example for Go\nprotoc --go_out=. --go-grpc_out=. \\\n  api/envoy/service/ext_proc/v3/external_processor.proto\n\n# Example for Java (Maven)\nmvn generate-sources\n```\n\nAfter regeneration, fix any compilation errors caused by the `ImmediateResponse.body` type change from `string` to `bytes`:\n\n**Go — before:**\n```go\nimmediateResponse := &ext_proc.ImmediateResponse{\n    Status: &envoy_type.HttpStatus{Code: envoy_type.StatusCode_Forbidden},\n    Body:   \"Access denied\",  // was string\n}\n```\n\n**Go — after:**\n```go\nimmediateResponse := &ext_proc.ImmediateResponse{\n    Status: &envoy_type.HttpStatus{Code: envoy_type.StatusCode_Forbidden},\n    Body:   []byte(\"Access denied\"),  // now bytes\n}\n```\n\n**Java — before:**\n```java\nImmediateResponse response = ImmediateResponse.newBuilder()\n    .setStatus(HttpStatus.newBuilder().setCode(StatusCode.Forbidden))\n    .setBody(\"Access denied\")  // was String\n    .build();\n```\n\n**Java — after:**\n```java\nImmediateResponse response = ImmediateResponse.newBuilder()\n    .setStatus(HttpStatus.newBuilder().setCode(StatusCode.Forbidden))\n    .setBody(ByteString.copyFromUtf8(\"Access denied\"))  // now ByteString\n    .build();\n```\n\n---\n\n### Step 2: Replace `disable_clear_route_cache` with `route_cache_action`\n\nSearch your Envoy configurations for `disable_clear_route_cache` and replace:\n\n```bash\n# Find all occurrences\ngrep -r \"disable_clear_route_cache\" /path/to/envoy/configs/\n```\n\nApply the following replacement mapping:\n\n| Old config | New config |\n|------------|------------|\n| `disable_clear_route_cache: true` | `route_cache_action: RETAIN` |\n| `disable_clear_route_cache: false` | Remove field (or `route_cache_action: DEFAULT`) |\n| _(field absent)_ | No change needed |\n\n**Full before/after example:**\n\nBefore (v1.30):\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: my_ext_proc\n    processing_mode:\n      request_header_mode: SEND\n      response_header_mode: SEND\n    disable_clear_route_cache: true\n    message_timeout: 0.2s\n```\n\nAfter (v1.31):\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: my_ext_proc\n    processing_mode:\n      request_header_mode: SEND\n      response_header_mode: SEND\n    route_cache_action: RETAIN\n    message_timeout: 0.2s\n```\n\n---\n\n### Step 3: Update Timeout Error Handling\n\nUpdate any downstream clients, retry policies, or monitoring rules that specifically detect ext_proc timeout failures via HTTP 500:\n\n```yaml\n# Envoy retry policy — update to include 504\nretry_policy:\n  retry_on: \"gateway-error,connect-failure,retriable-4xx\"\n  # \"gateway-error\" already covers 502, 503, 504 — no change needed\n  # But if you had custom logic for 500 specifically for ext_proc, update it\n```\n\nFor custom client-side logic:\n- Before: `if response.status_code == 500 and \"ext_proc timeout\" in response.body`\n- After: `if response.status_code == 504`\n\n---\n\n### Step 4: Audit CEL Attribute Expressions\n\nIf you use `request_attributes` or `response_attributes` with CEL expressions, audit any expressions that might return:\n- `null` values (were sent as `null_value`, now fall through to string)\n- Floating-point `double` values (were sent as `number_value`, now may fall through to string)\n- Integer values (were silently dropped, now correctly sent as `number_value`)\n\nTest your ext_proc server's attribute handling with representative requests to ensure the type changes do not cause parse failures.\n\n---\n\n### Step 5: Verify Route Cache Behavior (if using `clear_route_cache`)\n\nIf your ext_proc server sets `clear_route_cache: true` in responses, verify whether it always includes at least one header mutation in those responses:\n\n1. Enable debug logging: `ENVOY_LOG_LEVEL=debug`\n2. Look for log messages: `\"NOT clearing route cache. No header mutation in the response\"` — this indicates your ext_proc server is setting `clear_route_cache: true` on responses without mutations.\n3. Monitor the `clear_route_cache_ignored` stat counter — a non-zero value indicates affected requests.\n\n---\n\n### Step 6: Optionally Adopt Observability Mode\n\nIf you previously used a workaround for async/fire-and-forget processing, evaluate adopting `observability_mode: true`. Configure a suitable `deferred_close_timeout` based on your expected processing latency:\n\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: ext_proc_observer\n    observability_mode: true\n    deferred_close_timeout: 5s   # Tune based on your processor's latency\n    processing_mode:\n      request_header_mode: SEND\n      response_header_mode: SEND\n      request_body_mode: STREAMED    # Only STREAMED works in observability mode\n      response_body_mode: NONE       # NONE is fine too\n```\n\n---\n\n## Testing Recommendations\n\n### 1. Integration Test: `ImmediateResponse.body` Type Change\n\nWrite an integration test where your ext_proc server sends an `ImmediateResponse` with a non-empty body, and verify:\n- The downstream client receives the expected body content.\n- Non-UTF-8 binary body content is transmitted correctly (since `bytes` now allows this).\n\n### 2. Integration Test: Timeout Error Code\n\nSimulate an ext_proc server that does not respond within `message_timeout`:\n```yaml\nmessage_timeout: 0.1s  # 100ms — short enough to trigger easily in tests\n```\nVerify the downstream client receives **HTTP 504**, not HTTP 500.\n\n### 3. Unit Test: Route Cache Behavior\n\nFor each `route_cache_action` setting, test with:\n- An ext_proc response that sets `clear_route_cache: true` WITH a header mutation → cache should clear in `DEFAULT` and `CLEAR` modes.\n- An ext_proc response that sets `clear_route_cache: true` WITHOUT a header mutation → cache should NOT clear in `DEFAULT` mode; verify `clear_route_cache_ignored` stat increments.\n- `route_cache_action: RETAIN` → cache never clears; verify `clear_route_cache_disabled` stat increments when the response requests a clear.\n- `route_cache_action: CLEAR` → cache always clears when there are header mutations, regardless of the `clear_route_cache` flag.\n\n### 4. Stat Monitoring\n\nMonitor the following ext_proc stats to validate migration:\n\n| Stat | Expected behavior post-migration |\n|------|----------------------------------|\n| `ext_proc.<prefix>.clear_route_cache_disabled` | Increments when `route_cache_action: RETAIN` blocks a requested cache clear |\n| `ext_proc.<prefix>.clear_route_cache_ignored` | Increments when `DEFAULT` mode skips clearing due to no header mutations |\n| `ext_proc.<prefix>.clear_route_cache_upstream_ignored` | Increments when ext_proc is in upstream filter chain (cannot clear route cache) |\n\n### 5. Observability Mode Testing\n\nWhen testing `observability_mode`:\n- Verify that the filter chain does **not** pause at ext_proc processing points (response latency should not include ext_proc server latency).\n- Verify that your ext_proc server still receives all configured messages.\n- Test the `deferred_close_timeout` by measuring when the gRPC stream closes after the HTTP request completes.\n- Confirm that only `STREAMED` body mode messages are delivered (not `BUFFERED` or `BUFFERED_PARTIAL`).\n\n### 6. Runtime Flag Validation\n\nTest gRPC sidestream flow control behavior:\n```yaml\n# Test with flow control disabled\nlayered_runtime:\n  layers:\n  - name: static_layer\n    static_layer:\n      envoy.reloadable_features.grpc_side_stream_flow_control: false\n```\nCompare behavior with it enabled (default) to confirm no regressions.\n\n---\n\n## Rollback Guidance\n\nIf issues arise after upgrading to v1.31, here is how to revert specific changes:\n\n### Rollback: Route Cache Action\n\nThe `route_cache_action` field is new in v1.31. If rolling back to v1.30, remove `route_cache_action` from your config and restore `disable_clear_route_cache` if you need it:\n\n```yaml\n# v1.31 config\nroute_cache_action: RETAIN\n\n# v1.30 rollback config\ndisable_clear_route_cache: true\n```\n\nNote: v1.30 does not know about `route_cache_action` and will **reject the config** if that field is present. Remove it before rolling back.\n\n### Rollback: Timeout Error Code (504 vs 500)\n\nThere is no runtime flag to revert to the 500 behavior. If your clients or SLOs critically depend on the 500 code, you must pin to v1.30 until they are updated.\n\n### Rollback: gRPC Sidestream Flow Control\n\nDisable the new flow control feature while remaining on v1.31:\n```yaml\nlayered_runtime:\n  layers:\n  - name: static_layer\n    static_layer:\n      envoy.reloadable_features.grpc_side_stream_flow_control: false\n```\n\n### Rollback: `ImmediateResponse.body` Type Change\n\nIf your ext_proc server's generated code cannot be updated before the rollback window closes, pin to v1.30's protobuf definitions. In most protobuf implementations, a `string` field and a `bytes` field with the same field number are wire-compatible (both encoded as length-delimited), so a v1.30 server sending a `string` body will be received correctly by v1.31 Envoy, and vice versa. However, the generated API types will mismatch and cause compilation errors, requiring code changes.\n\n### Rollback: Observability Mode\n\nIf `deferred_close_timeout` is causing streams to linger too long:\n- Decrease `deferred_close_timeout` (minimum: any positive duration).\n- Disable `observability_mode` entirely until root cause is identified.\n\n### General Rollback Procedure\n\n1. Remove any v1.31-only config fields (`route_cache_action`, `deferred_close_timeout`) before rolling back the binary.\n2. Restore `disable_clear_route_cache` if previously used.\n3. Roll back the Envoy binary to v1.30.\n4. Roll back ext_proc server generated code to v1.30 proto definitions.\n5. Verify the `clear_route_cache_ignored` stat is no longer incrementing unexpectedly.\n\n---\n\n## Appendix: Changed Files Reference\n\n| File | Type of Change |\n|------|---------------|\n| `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto` | Added `RouteCacheAction` enum, `route_cache_action` field (18), `deferred_close_timeout` field (19); The release adds two major new capabilities — **Observability Mode** and the **`route_cache_action` enum** — while also changing the HTTP timeout error response code, introducing gRPC sidestream flow control, and making a wire-level type change in the service API for `ImmediateResponse.body`.\n\nMost operators will need to update their configurations to use `route_cache_action` in place of `disable_clear_route_cache`, and should be aware that ext_proc timeout errors now return HTTP 504 instead of 500.\n\n### Summary of Major Changes\n\n| Area | Change Type | Impact |\n|------|-------------|--------|\n| `ImmediateResponse.body` type changed from `string` to `bytes` | Breaking (proto wire) | Ext_proc server implementations must update generated code |\n| `disable_clear_route_cache` deprecated in favor of `route_cache_action` enum | Deprecation + behavior change | Config update recommended; cannot use both simultaneously |\n| Timeout errors now return HTTP 504 Gateway Timeout instead of 500 | Behavior change | Update client error-handling and alerting |\n| New `observability_mode` field (supersedes `async_mode`) | New feature | Optional adoption |\n| New `deferred_close_timeout` field for observability mode | New feature | Optional adoption |\n| New `route_cache_action` enum with `DEFAULT`, `CLEAR`, `RETAIN` values | New feature + deprecation | Replaces `disable_clear_route_cache` |\n| gRPC sidestream flow control via runtime flag | New feature | Enabled by default; can be disabled |\n| CEL attribute type handling changed (`kInt64`/`kUint64` replace `kNullType`/`kDouble`) | Behavior change | Affects `request_attributes`/`response_attributes` users |\n| Tracing context propagation to ext_proc gRPC streams | Bug fix / new behavior | No config change required |\n\n---\n\n## Breaking Changes\n\n### 1. `ImmediateResponse.body` Type Changed: `string` → `bytes`\n\n**File:** `api/envoy/service/ext_proc/v3/external_processor.proto`, line 342\n\nThe `body` field in the `ImmediateResponse` message changed its protobuf type from `string` to `bytes`.\n\n**v1.30 proto definition:**\n```protobuf\nmessage ImmediateResponse {\n  type.v3.HttpStatus status = 1;\n  HeaderMutation headers = 2;\n  // The message body to return with the response.\n  string body = 3;       // <-- was string\n  GrpcStatus grpc_status = 4;\n  string details = 5;\n}\n```\n\n**v1.31 proto definition:**\n```protobuf\nmessage ImmediateResponse {\n  type.v3.HttpStatus status = 1;\n  HeaderMutation headers = 2;\n  // The message body to return with the response.\n  bytes body = 3;        // <-- now bytes\n  GrpcStatus grpc_status = 4;\n  string details = 5;\n}\n```\n\n**Impact:** Although protobuf field number 3 remains the same (wire-compatible at the encoding level), the generated code in your ext_proc server will change type. In most languages:\n\n- **Go:** The field type changes from `string` to `[]byte`. Assignment code like `resp.Body = \"error message\"` must become `resp.Body = []byte(\"error message\")`.\n- **Java:** Changes from `String` to `ByteString`. Use `ByteString.copyFromUtf8(\"error message\")`.\n- **Python:** Changes from `str` to `bytes`. Use `b\"error message\"`.\n- **C++:** Changes from `std::string` to a bytes-typed field (still `std::string` in C++ protobuf, but semantically different).\n\n**Motivation:** HTTP response bodies are arbitrary binary content, not necessarily valid UTF-8. Using `bytes` is more correct and removes ambiguity.\n\n**Action Required:** Regenerate your gRPC/protobuf client stubs from the updated `.proto` files and update any ext_proc server code that sets `ImmediateResponse.body`.\n\n---\n\n### 2. `disable_clear_route_cache` and `route_cache_action` Cannot Be Set Simultaneously\n\n**File:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.cc`\n\nIn v1.31, if both `disable_clear_route_cache` (set to `true`) and `route_cache_action` (set to any non-`DEFAULT` value) are present in the same `ExternalProcessor` configuration, Envoy will **throw a configuration error at startup**:\n\n```\nEnvoyException: disable_clear_route_cache and route_cache_action can not be set to none-default at the same time.\n```\n\n**v1.30 config (valid in v1.30, throws in v1.31 if both non-default):**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: ext_proc_cluster\n    disable_clear_route_cache: true\n    route_cache_action: RETAIN   # CONFLICT: both fields now active\n```\n\n**Action Required:** Use only one of the two fields. See the [Deprecated Features](#deprecated-features) section for the recommended migration path.\n\n---\n\n## Deprecated Features\n\n### 1. `disable_clear_route_cache` Deprecated in Favor of `route_cache_action`\n\n**Proto field:** `ExternalProcessor.disable_clear_route_cache` (field 11)\n**Replacement:** `ExternalProcessor.route_cache_action` (field 18, new in v1.31)\n**Files:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`, `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/processor_state.cc`\n\nThe boolean `disable_clear_route_cache` field is now annotated for future promotion to a `oneof` and is superseded by the new `route_cache_action` enum. The field still works in v1.31 but Envoy internally converts it: if `disable_clear_route_cache: true` is set, it is treated as `route_cache_action: RETAIN`.\n\nThe new `RouteCacheAction` enum provides three options:\n\n| Value | Behavior |\n|-------|----------|\n| `DEFAULT` (0) | Clear the route cache only when the ext_proc response sets `clear_route_cache: true` AND there is a header mutation in the response. This is the default. |\n| `CLEAR` (1) | Always clear the route cache whenever there is a header mutation, regardless of the `clear_route_cache` flag in the ext_proc response. |\n| `RETAIN` (2) | Never clear the route cache. Equivalent to the old `disable_clear_route_cache: true`. Increments the `clear_route_cache_disabled` stat if the response tried to clear it. |\n\nAdditionally, in v1.31 the `DEFAULT` behavior itself changed slightly: the route cache is now only cleared when **both** the ext_proc response's `clear_route_cache` field is `true` **and** there is at least one header mutation. In v1.30, any response with `clear_route_cache: true` (even without header mutations) would trigger a cache clear.\n\n**v1.30 config:**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: ext_proc_cluster\n    disable_clear_route_cache: true\n```\n\n**v1.31 recommended config (equivalent behavior):**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: ext_proc_cluster\n    route_cache_action: RETAIN\n```\n\n**Migration advice:**\n- Replace `disable_clear_route_cache: true` → `route_cache_action: RETAIN`\n- Replace `disable_clear_route_cache: false` (or omitting the field) → `route_cache_action: DEFAULT` (or omit, same result)\n- To always force a route re-computation on header mutations: use `route_cache_action: CLEAR`\n\n---\n\n## New Features\n\n### 1. Observability Mode (`observability_mode`)\n\n**Proto field:** `ExternalProcessor.observability_mode` (field 17, existed in v1.30 but formalized in v1.31)\n**New field:** `ExternalProcessor.deferred_close_timeout` (field 19, new in v1.31)\n**Files:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`, `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.h`, `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.cc`\n\nObservability mode (also called \"Send and Go\" mode) allows the ext_proc filter to forward all configured processing steps to the external processor **without pausing the filter chain**. Envoy sends each message and immediately continues processing — it does not wait for a response. This is the recommended replacement for the unimplemented `async_mode` concept.\n\n**Characteristics of observability mode:**\n1. Only `STREAMED` body processing mode is supported. Other body modes (`BUFFERED`, `BUFFERED_PARTIAL`) are silently ignored. `NONE` (skip body) still works.\n2. The external processor should not send responses; any responses sent are ignored.\n3. `message_timeout` does not apply in this mode.\n4. The external processor may close the stream to signal it no longer needs data.\n5. The gRPC stream is closed **after a deferred timeout** (default: 5 seconds) rather than immediately on filter destruction, to allow in-flight messages to drain.\n\n**New `deferred_close_timeout` field:**\n\nIn v1.31, when `observability_mode` is `true`, the gRPC stream to the ext_proc server is not closed immediately when the HTTP request/response completes. Instead, it is closed after a configurable delay, giving the ext_proc server time to receive and process all buffered messages.\n\nDefault value: **5000 milliseconds (5 seconds)**\n\n**Example configuration:**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: ext_proc_observer_cluster\n    observability_mode: true\n    deferred_close_timeout: 10s    # Allow 10s for stream drain (default: 5s)\n    processing_mode:\n      request_header_mode: SEND\n      response_header_mode: SEND\n      request_body_mode: STREAMED  # Only STREAMED is supported in observability mode\n      response_body_mode: STREAMED\n```\n\n**Flow control warning:** Flow control to prevent fast senders from overwhelming a slower ext_proc server in observability mode is not yet fully implemented. Use this feature at your own discretion, especially under high traffic. (Tracked at [envoy issue #33319](https://github.com/envoyproxy/envoy/issues/33319).)\n\n---\n\n### 2. `route_cache_action` Enum\n\n**Proto field:** `ExternalProcessor.route_cache_action` (field 18)\n**File:** `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto`\n\nSee the [Deprecated Features](#deprecated-features) section for full details. The key new capability is `CLEAR` mode, which lets you configure ext_proc to always re-evaluate routes after header modifications, without requiring the ext_proc server to set the `clear_route_cache` flag in each response.\n\n**Example — always clear route cache:**\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: ext_proc_cluster\n    route_cache_action: CLEAR\n```\n\n---\n\n### 3. gRPC Sidestream Flow Control\n\n**Runtime flag:** `envoy.reloadable_features.grpc_side_stream_flow_control`\n**Files:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/client_impl.h`, `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/client_impl.cc`, `source/common/runtime/runtime_features.cc`\n\nv1.31 adds flow control support to the bidirectional gRPC stream between Envoy and the ext_proc server. When enabled (default: `true`), Envoy sets watermark callbacks on the gRPC stream and removes them when the stream closes, preventing the ext_proc stream from being flooded with data faster than the external server can process it.\n\nThis feature is enabled by default and requires no configuration. It can be disabled via the runtime flag if it causes issues:\n\n```yaml\n# Bootstrap config to disable (if needed)\nlayered_runtime:\n  layers:\n  - name: static_layer\n    static_layer:\n      envoy.reloadable_features.grpc_side_stream_flow_control: false\n```\n\n---\n\n### 4. Tracing Context Propagation to ext_proc gRPC Streams\n\n**File:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.cc`\n**Changelog:** `changelogs/current.yaml` (minor behavior change)\n\nIn v1.31, tracing context headers (e.g., `traceparent`, `x-b3-traceid`) are now correctly propagated when Envoy opens the gRPC stream to the ext_proc server. In v1.30 and earlier, these headers were not set on the gRPC sidestream connection.\n\nThis change affects both the Envoy gRPC client and the Google C++ gRPC client. No configuration changes are required, but ext_proc server operators may now observe tracing headers that were previously absent. This enables distributed tracing to span the ext_proc side-channel.\n\n---\n\n### 5. New Test Infrastructure: Tracing Test Filter\n\n**Files:** `test/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/tracer_test_filter.cc`, `test/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/tracer_test_filter.proto` (new in v1.31)\n\nA new tracing test filter and matching utilities test (`matching_utils_test.cc`) have been added. These are test-only components and do not affect production behavior, but signal that tracing integration is now formally tested for ext_proc.\n\n---\n\n## Behavioral Changes\n\n### 1. Timeout Errors Now Return HTTP 504 Instead of HTTP 500\n\n**File:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/ext_proc.cc`\n**Changelog:** `changelogs/current.yaml`, minor behavior change section\n\nWhen the ext_proc filter's `message_timeout` expires before the external processor responds, Envoy now sends an HTTP **504 Gateway Timeout** response to the downstream client. Previously, this error returned HTTP **500 Internal Server Error**.\n\n**Impact:**\n- Downstream clients that check for `500` errors to detect ext_proc timeout failures must now also check (or switch to checking) for `504`.\n- Alert rules and dashboards monitoring for `5xx` errors are not affected — both codes are counted as server errors.\n- The `RESPONSE_CODE_DETAILS` access log field for these errors will reflect the gateway timeout.\n\n**No configuration change is required**, but you should:\n- Update any client-side retry logic that specifically matches on HTTP 500 for ext_proc timeouts.\n- Update monitoring dashboards and SLO definitions that specifically count ext_proc-induced HTTP 500s.\n\n---\n\n### 2. Route Cache Clear Now Requires Header Mutation (DEFAULT mode)\n\n**File:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/processor_state.cc`\n\nIn v1.30, if an ext_proc response set `clear_route_cache: true`, the route cache was cleared regardless of whether the response also contained a header mutation.\n\nIn v1.31 with `route_cache_action: DEFAULT` (the default), the route cache is **only cleared when both conditions are true:**\n1. The ext_proc response sets `clear_route_cache: true`, **and**\n2. The response includes at least one header mutation (`has_header_mutation()` returns true).\n\nIf an ext_proc response sets `clear_route_cache: true` but has no header mutations, the cache clear is skipped and the `clear_route_cache_ignored` counter is incremented.\n\n**Motivation:** Clearing the route cache is only meaningful when headers have changed. A response requesting cache clear with no mutations is likely a bug in the ext_proc server.\n\n**Action Required:** If your ext_proc server sets `clear_route_cache: true` on responses without header mutations, you need to either:\n- Add a header mutation to the response (even a no-op one), or\n- Switch to `route_cache_action: CLEAR` to always clear on header mutations regardless of the flag, or\n- Fix the ext_proc server to not set `clear_route_cache` when there are no header mutations.\n\n---\n\n### 3. CEL Attribute Value Type Handling Changed\n\n**File:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/matching_utils.cc`\n\nThe CEL expression evaluation for `request_attributes` and `response_attributes` changed how it maps CEL value types to protobuf `Value` types:\n\n| CEL Type | v1.30 behavior | v1.31 behavior |\n|----------|---------------|---------------|\n| `kNullType` | Sets `null_value` in proto Value | **Removed** — no longer handled |\n| `kDouble` | Sets `number_value` via `DoubleOrDie()` | **Removed** — replaced by integer handling |\n| `kInt64` | Not explicitly handled (fell through) | Sets `number_value` via `Int64OrDie()` |\n| `kUint64` | Not explicitly handled (fell through) | Sets `number_value` via `Uint64OrDie()` |\n\n**Impact:**\n- Attributes with CEL expressions that evaluate to `null` or floating-point `double` values will no longer produce `number_value` or `null_value` fields in the `attributes` map. They will fall through to string conversion instead.\n- Attributes that evaluate to `int64` or `uint64` values now correctly appear as `number_value` in the attributes map sent to the ext_proc server.\n\nThis is primarily relevant for operators using numeric or null-returning CEL expressions in `request_attributes` or `response_attributes`.\n\n---\n\n### 4. Stream Lifecycle and `notifyFilterDestroy()`\n\n**Files:** `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/client.h`, `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/client_impl.h`, `source/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/client_impl.cc`\n\nInternally, the gRPC stream between Envoy and the ext_proc server is now managed by a thread-local `ThreadLocalStreamManager` rather than being owned directly by the filter instance as a `unique_ptr`. The `ExternalProcessorStream` interface gained a new `notifyFilterDestroy()` method that must be called when the filter is destroyed, to prevent the stream from making callbacks into a destroyed filter object.\n\nThe `callbacks_` member in `ExternalProcessorStreamImpl` changed from a raw reference (`ExternalProcessorCallbacks&`) to an optional reference (`OptRef<ExternalProcessorCallbacks>`), which is reset to empty when `notifyFilterDestroy()` is called.\n\n**Impact on custom ext_proc client implementations:** If you have implemented a custom `ExternalProcessorClient` (unlikely for most operators, but possible for testing), you must implement the new `notifyFilterDestroy()` pure virtual method.\n\n---\n\n## Migration Steps\n\n### Step 1: Update Protobuf Generated Code\n\nRe-generate your ext_proc server's gRPC stubs from the updated `.proto` files:\n\n```bash\n# Example for Go\nprotoc --go_out=. --go-grpc_out=. \\\n  api/envoy/service/ext_proc/v3/external_processor.proto\n\n# Example for Java (Maven)\nmvn generate-sources\n```\n\nAfter regeneration, fix any compilation errors caused by the `ImmediateResponse.body` type change from `string` to `bytes`:\n\n**Go — before:**\n```go\nimmediateResponse := &ext_proc.ImmediateResponse{\n    Status: &envoy_type.HttpStatus{Code: envoy_type.StatusCode_Forbidden},\n    Body:   \"Access denied\",  // was string\n}\n```\n\n**Go — after:**\n```go\nimmediateResponse := &ext_proc.ImmediateResponse{\n    Status: &envoy_type.HttpStatus{Code: envoy_type.StatusCode_Forbidden},\n    Body:   []byte(\"Access denied\"),  // now bytes\n}\n```\n\n**Java — before:**\n```java\nImmediateResponse response = ImmediateResponse.newBuilder()\n    .setStatus(HttpStatus.newBuilder().setCode(StatusCode.Forbidden))\n    .setBody(\"Access denied\")  // was String\n    .build();\n```\n\n**Java — after:**\n```java\nImmediateResponse response = ImmediateResponse.newBuilder()\n    .setStatus(HttpStatus.newBuilder().setCode(StatusCode.Forbidden))\n    .setBody(ByteString.copyFromUtf8(\"Access denied\"))  // now ByteString\n    .build();\n```\n\n---\n\n### Step 2: Replace `disable_clear_route_cache` with `route_cache_action`\n\nSearch your Envoy configurations for `disable_clear_route_cache` and replace:\n\n```bash\n# Find all occurrences\ngrep -r \"disable_clear_route_cache\" /path/to/envoy/configs/\n```\n\nApply the following replacement mapping:\n\n| Old config | New config |\n|------------|------------|\n| `disable_clear_route_cache: true` | `route_cache_action: RETAIN` |\n| `disable_clear_route_cache: false` | Remove field (or `route_cache_action: DEFAULT`) |\n| _(field absent)_ | No change needed |\n\n**Full before/after example:**\n\nBefore (v1.30):\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: my_ext_proc\n    processing_mode:\n      request_header_mode: SEND\n      response_header_mode: SEND\n    disable_clear_route_cache: true\n    message_timeout: 0.2s\n```\n\nAfter (v1.31):\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: my_ext_proc\n    processing_mode:\n      request_header_mode: SEND\n      response_header_mode: SEND\n    route_cache_action: RETAIN\n    message_timeout: 0.2s\n```\n\n---\n\n### Step 3: Update Timeout Error Handling\n\nUpdate any downstream clients, retry policies, or monitoring rules that specifically detect ext_proc timeout failures via HTTP 500:\n\n```yaml\n# Envoy retry policy — update to include 504\nretry_policy:\n  retry_on: \"gateway-error,connect-failure,retriable-4xx\"\n  # \"gateway-error\" already covers 502, 503, 504 — no change needed\n  # But if you had custom logic for 500 specifically for ext_proc, update it\n```\n\nFor custom client-side logic:\n- Before: `if response.status_code == 500 and \"ext_proc timeout\" in response.body`\n- After: `if response.status_code == 504`\n\n---\n\n### Step 4: Audit CEL Attribute Expressions\n\nIf you use `request_attributes` or `response_attributes` with CEL expressions, audit any expressions that might return:\n- `null` values (were sent as `null_value`, now fall through to string)\n- Floating-point `double` values (were sent as `number_value`, now may fall through to string)\n- Integer values (were silently dropped, now correctly sent as `number_value`)\n\nTest your ext_proc server's attribute handling with representative requests to ensure the type changes do not cause parse failures.\n\n---\n\n### Step 5: Verify Route Cache Behavior (if using `clear_route_cache`)\n\nIf your ext_proc server sets `clear_route_cache: true` in responses, verify whether it always includes at least one header mutation in those responses:\n\n1. Enable debug logging: `ENVOY_LOG_LEVEL=debug`\n2. Look for log messages: `\"NOT clearing route cache. No header mutation in the response\"` — this indicates your ext_proc server is setting `clear_route_cache: true` on responses without mutations.\n3. Monitor the `clear_route_cache_ignored` stat counter — a non-zero value indicates affected requests.\n\n---\n\n### Step 6: Optionally Adopt Observability Mode\n\nIf you previously used a workaround for async/fire-and-forget processing, evaluate adopting `observability_mode: true`. Configure a suitable `deferred_close_timeout` based on your expected processing latency:\n\n```yaml\nhttp_filters:\n- name: envoy.filters.http.ext_proc\n  typed_config:\n    \"@type\": type.googleapis.com/envoy.extensions.filters.http.ext_proc.v3.ExternalProcessor\n    grpc_service:\n      envoy_grpc:\n        cluster_name: ext_proc_observer\n    observability_mode: true\n    deferred_close_timeout: 5s   # Tune based on your processor's latency\n    processing_mode:\n      request_header_mode: SEND\n      response_header_mode: SEND\n      request_body_mode: STREAMED    # Only STREAMED works in observability mode\n      response_body_mode: NONE       # NONE is fine too\n```\n\n---\n\n## Testing Recommendations\n\n### 1. Integration Test: `ImmediateResponse.body` Type Change\n\nWrite an integration test where your ext_proc server sends an `ImmediateResponse` with a non-empty body, and verify:\n- The downstream client receives the expected body content.\n- Non-UTF-8 binary body content is transmitted correctly (since `bytes` now allows this).\n\n### 2. Integration Test: Timeout Error Code\n\nSimulate an ext_proc server that does not respond within `message_timeout`:\n```yaml\nmessage_timeout: 0.1s  # 100ms — short enough to trigger easily in tests\n```\nVerify the downstream client receives **HTTP 504**, not HTTP 500.\n\n### 3. Unit Test: Route Cache Behavior\n\nFor each `route_cache_action` setting, test with:\n- An ext_proc response that sets `clear_route_cache: true` WITH a header mutation → cache should clear in `DEFAULT` and `CLEAR` modes.\n- An ext_proc response that sets `clear_route_cache: true` WITHOUT a header mutation → cache should NOT clear in `DEFAULT` mode; verify `clear_route_cache_ignored` stat increments.\n- `route_cache_action: RETAIN` → cache never clears; verify `clear_route_cache_disabled` stat increments when the response requests a clear.\n- `route_cache_action: CLEAR` → cache always clears when there are header mutations, regardless of the `clear_route_cache` flag.\n\n### 4. Stat Monitoring\n\nMonitor the following ext_proc stats to validate migration:\n\n| Stat | Expected behavior post-migration |\n|------|----------------------------------|\n| `ext_proc.<prefix>.clear_route_cache_disabled` | Increments when `route_cache_action: RETAIN` blocks a requested cache clear |\n| `ext_proc.<prefix>.clear_route_cache_ignored` | Increments when `DEFAULT` mode skips clearing due to no header mutations |\n| `ext_proc.<prefix>.clear_route_cache_upstream_ignored` | Increments when ext_proc is in upstream filter chain (cannot clear route cache) |\n\n### 5. Observability Mode Testing\n\nWhen testing `observability_mode`:\n- Verify that the filter chain does **not** pause at ext_proc processing points (response latency should not include ext_proc server latency).\n- Verify that your ext_proc server still receives all configured messages.\n- Test the `deferred_close_timeout` by measuring when the gRPC stream closes after the HTTP request completes.\n- Confirm that only `STREAMED` body mode messages are delivered (not `BUFFERED` or `BUFFERED_PARTIAL`).\n\n### 6. Runtime Flag Validation\n\nTest gRPC sidestream flow control behavior:\n```yaml\n# Test with flow control disabled\nlayered_runtime:\n  layers:\n  - name: static_layer\n    static_layer:\n      envoy.reloadable_features.grpc_side_stream_flow_control: false\n```\nCompare behavior with it enabled (default) to confirm no regressions.\n\n---\n\n## Rollback Guidance\n\nIf issues arise after upgrading to v1.31, here is how to revert specific changes:\n\n### Rollback: Route Cache Action\n\nThe `route_cache_action` field is new in v1.31. If rolling back to v1.30, remove `route_cache_action` from your config and restore `disable_clear_route_cache` if you need it:\n\n```yaml\n# v1.31 config\nroute_cache_action: RETAIN\n\n# v1.30 rollback config\ndisable_clear_route_cache: true\n```\n\nNote: v1.30 does not know about `route_cache_action` and will **reject the config** if that field is present. Remove it before rolling back.\n\n### Rollback: Timeout Error Code (504 vs 500)\n\nThere is no runtime flag to revert to the 500 behavior. If your clients or SLOs critically depend on the 500 code, you must pin to v1.30 until they are updated.\n\n### Rollback: gRPC Sidestream Flow Control\n\nDisable the new flow control feature while remaining on v1.31:\n```yaml\nlayered_runtime:\n  layers:\n  - name: static_layer\n    static_layer:\n      envoy.reloadable_features.grpc_side_stream_flow_control: false\n```\n\n### Rollback: `ImmediateResponse.body` Type Change\n\nIf your ext_proc server's generated code cannot be updated before the rollback window closes, pin to v1.30's protobuf definitions. In most protobuf implementations, a `string` field and a `bytes` field with the same field number are wire-compatible (both encoded as length-delimited), so a v1.30 server sending a `string` body will be received correctly by v1.31 Envoy, and vice versa. However, the generated API types will mismatch and cause compilation errors, requiring code changes.\n\n### Rollback: Observability Mode\n\nIf `deferred_close_timeout` is causing streams to linger too long:\n- Decrease `deferred_close_timeout` (minimum: any positive duration).\n- Disable `observability_mode` entirely until root cause is identified.\n\n### General Rollback Procedure\n\n1. Remove any v1.31-only config fields (`route_cache_action`, `deferred_close_timeout`) before rolling back the binary.\n2. Restore `disable_clear_route_cache` if previously used.\n3. Roll back the Envoy binary to v1.30.\n4. Roll back ext_proc server generated code to v1.30 proto definitions.\n5. Verify the `clear_route_cache_ignored` stat is no longer incrementing unexpectedly.\n\n---\n\n## Appendix: Changed Files Reference\n\n| File | Type of Change |\n|------|---------------|\n| `api/envoy/extensions/filters/http/ext_proc/v3/ext_proc.proto` | Added `RouteCacheAction` enum, `route_cache_action` field (18), `deferred_close_timeout` field (19);
